ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u12	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! The government's decision to modernise the railway lines forming the Eastern Railway Magistrala is of great importance to the inhabitants of the Eastern Poland voivodships. Thanks to the implementation of a number of investments in railway infrastructure, a modern WMK will be created connecting the voivodship cities of eastern Poland: Olsztyn - Białystok - Lublin - Kielce - Rzeszów. The creation of a single railway route is an opportunity to exit from the economic hole, as this area was greatly neglected during the PO-PSL coalition's reign due to the electoral preferences of its inhabitants. The bus is to connect the least developed regions of our country, which will open up investment opportunities for them. This will increase the economic competitiveness of the macro-region, which will allow faster economic growth and improve the quality of life of the inhabitants. Investment activities of PLN 6.5 billion will be undertaken in the coming years, according to the assumptions. This is an opportunity to increase the share of rail transport in freight transport, which is expected by both entrepreneurs and agricultural producers. Citizens are also waiting for the creation of a convenient network of passenger connections, for which modernised lines will mean a radical shortening of travel times. Rail transport will lighten the road, reduce the negative impact of road transport on the environment. I am particularly pleased with the announcement of the government about the priority treatment of investments in eastern voivodships, because here the negligence was the greatest, as on line 25 from Sandomierz to Skarżyska-Kamienna, which will constitute an important route of the Eastern Railway Bus. It is in Świętokrzyskie voivodship that the most important industrial plants are located along the railway line - the arms industry in Skarżysk-Kamienna, numerous plants in the thriving special economic zone in Starachowice, MAN plants, the steel industry in Ostrowiec Świętokrzyskie or the glassworks in Sandomierz. Modernization of the line will allow you to improve logistics in transport and production and transfer transport from roads to tracks. It's also a huge opportunity for food producers. Work on the Białystok - Suwałki - Trakiszko railway line - the state border, is being analysed by the possibility of dividing the project into two stages Białystok - Ełk and Elk - Trakiszko. The preliminary analysis showed the validity of the application under the current CEF competition, the Connecting Europe Facility, the first stage, i.e. Białystok - Ełk, the second stage notification Ełk - Trakiszka is planned in the next CEF competition after carrying out additional studies. Work on railway line No 7 Warszawa Wschodnia Obsobowa - Dorohusk at the section Warsaw - Otwock - Dęblin - Lublin, the project will be carried out within the framework of the OP IiŚ 2014. As part of the above project, the eastern bus includes the section Dęblin - Lublin, and the tender for the selection of the contractor of the above section is currently ongoing. The signing of works contracts is planned in the first quarter of 2017, completion of works in 2020. Elektrification of the line Ocice - Rzeszów. In June this year a tender was launched, which includes preparatory work for electrification of the whole section of Rzeszów - Ocice. These two tasks: rebuilding and securing existing electricity and telecommunications networks so as not to interfere with the planned investment and logging of trees, construction works are planned for the years 2017 and 2020. Most of the bus routes are planned to achieve the following parameters: maximum speed - 120 km/h for passenger sections and 100 km/h for freight trains, permissible axle pressure - 221 kN and increased capacity by modernising rail traffic control devices. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, With regard to notification, the list of names of individual movements or mines presented by the European Commission is, on the one hand, linked to the decisions to transfer entities to the SRK, as the Member rightly pointed out, Jas-Mos or the Movement Anna. The notification shall also cover decisions which have been taken in previous years, from 2007 to 2016, and thereafter - 2018. As regards the Sośnica mine and the Silesian mine, because this year, last year too, for these mines was the year when they achieved a negative result, in the reporting that was presented to the European Commission, these mines were included in the programme and in January we will decide how long Sośnica, as part of its programme which is currently underway, is beginning to realise the possibilities of exiting the technically and economically difficult situation. As far as the Silesian mine is concerned, we also originally considered that the extraction of the walls that are currently armed should take place around the six-month period, but perhaps this period will shift. Hence, the dates that appeared there were due to the estimates that we submitted during the period until September this year. As for these two mines, as I mentioned, we will continue to talk in the context of assessing the situation - one in January and the other in Silesia, in December this year and in the beginning of the future. As for the Makoszowy mine, we have a decision from the European Commission that the financing for the activity which today is not profitable and has not been profitable for the previous years either, in 2015 and 2016, makes it possible for the mine to operate since 1 January but without the aid for public aid. The European Union has made a decision - we have still negotiated in the last days before this decision - in view of the fact that we are conducting a restructuring process and we are introducing certain programmes related to, for example, the regulation of the sales market and the market for individual operators in the hard coal sector. It was found, among others. also through the OKD complaint that we are placing subsidised coal on the market. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u32	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! (PL) Mr President, Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, In view of the fact that OC and AC motor insurance represent a significant part of the business of property insurance undertakings, the Financial Supervision Commission pays particular attention to the profitability in this area of insurance. In view of the fact that this activity has caused a technical loss in recent years, KNF called on insurance undertakings on 28 September 2015 to correctly determine the insurance premium, i.e. in such a way as to ensure that it is adequate to the obligations under the insurance contract and to the costs of the insurance business performed. The supervisory authority recommended insurance undertakings, inter alia.: review and change of premium tariffs in line with current incurred risks and costs per sales segment, including in the calculation of the cost premiums related to changes in injury elimination, review of policies on the applied discount schemes, increase of the insurance premium. The supervisory authority in the call expected insurance undertakings to take the above actions without undue delay, ensuring a long-term adjustment to the requirements of the laws of the motor vehicle owners' liability. The legal provisions provide that, in accordance with the Article. Article 32 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 11 September 2015 on Insurance and Insurance Activities, the insurance undertaking shall determine the amount of insurance premiums after the assessment of the insurance risk. The insurance premium shall be set at a rate which ensures at least the performance of all insurance contract obligations and the cost of carrying out the insurance undertaking's business and according to the criteria set out in the general insurance conditions, in particular as regards discounts or increases in the basic amounts. The insurance undertaking is required to collect appropriate statistics to determine the amount of insurance premiums and technical provisions on their basis. In addition, on the basis of Article. 8 Act of 22 May 2003 on compulsory insurance, Insurance Fund Guarantee and the Polish Office of Communication Insurances tariff and the amount of insurance premiums for compulsory insurance shall be determined by the insurance undertaking. However, in any event, the establishment of the compulsory insurance premiums tariff or their modification shall provide the supervisory authority with information on those tariffs and on the basis of their determination within 14 days of the date on which the earliest possible conclusion of the compulsory insurance contract with the premium calculated on the basis of them was possible on a new basis. This information should include, in particular, an analysis of the damage and the cost of providing insurance to justify any change in the tariff. The primary and main task of the communication OC insurance is to protect potentially injured third parties. Rules of law introducing, inter alia, the possibility of seeking compensation for the death of a loved one with a limitation period of up to 20 years, and recent developments in the case law of the Supreme Court are therefore aimed at ensuring the fullest possible protection for victims of accidents or persons entitled to compensation. This in turn results in an increase in compensation payments. The amount of compensation for personal injury and the death of the nearest person, the amount of the pension granted or the reimbursement of the rental costs of the replacement vehicle is also increasing. The so-called activity also remains important. compensation firms providing legal assistance to victims of claims against insurance undertakings. In addition, a draft bill was recently prepared by the Senate of the Republic of Poland aimed at introducing the liability of the Insurance Guarantee Fund for damage to accidents from the 1990s, those of the last century, where the guarantee amount has already been exhausted and this guarantee fund is financed by insurance companies. All this increases the value of compensation and other benefits paid by insurers. The fact that in recent years there has been a lack of balance between the contributions charged under the communication liability insurance contracts with the compensations paid and the operating costs of insurance undertakings shows the technical result in this insurance. In recent years it is definitely negative, which means that insurance companies are losing out on this activity. In recent years, this loss has evolved as follows. For three quarters of 2016, i.e. now, there are 806 million losses, in 2015 there were PLN 1056 million losses, in 2014 - PLN 782 million losses, etc. So for the last 6 years, the loss in this insurance amounted to over PLN 4 billion. Losses reported by insurance undertakings mean that the premiums collected in this insurance were not sufficient to cover all insurance obligations and to cover the costs of carrying out insurance activities. As far as AC insurance is concerned, the cumulative technical result in this insurance for the last 6 years is positive and amounts to PLN 1072 million. In this insurance there was a decrease in technical profit and a one-year loss of PLN 113 million in 2015, i.e. a total loss of PLN 2368 million in these two OC and AC insurances for 6 years. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u40	Thank you, Marshal. In fact, I could answer very briefly: no, no, no, no, no and no, but I'm going to try to develop it, okay? I know there are a lot of misunderstandings around this center. Unfortunately, the discussion on this issue was on the criticisms of public media towards various NGOs and also on my nomination. I've even been accused of being behind these publications, so at first I'm going to say that I had absolutely nothing to do with it. Besides, we talked about this at the conference with Prime Minister Gliński. I don't know if you've heard, but it's been explained. I will now try to say a few words about the centre to clarify these misunderstandings, and then I will address specific questions. In Poland, the entity responsible for implementing and promoting the solutions adopted in the Act on Public Benefit and Volunteering is the Department of Public Benefit under the authority of the Minister responsible for Social Security, i.e. the Minister of the Family. Such a solution makes it possible for the sector to be perceived mainly by social assistance and organisations providing it. In the meantime, the body responsible for the development of the sector should be given an interest in a multi-dimensional set of strategic challenges, including: issues such as guarding, spokes, civic and historical education, volunteering, civic diplomacy, the development of civil media, etc. Currently, there is a lack of an institution that collects data from all ministries and has full information on the extent and types of state support for the development of the third sector, which would allow for analysis and better evidence-based decisions. The first step in this direction was the appointment of a representative of the civil society government, whose tasks include: preparing the draft of this Act. Although I have become a proxy, as this project has been refined, of course I am jointly and severally taking responsibility for this project. The proposed new institution will take over the tasks of the Public Use Department, extending them to new priorities. This solution will provide a horizontal perspective on coordination and monitoring, but not centralisation - there is absolutely no centralisation here, and I will return to that - state support policies for the non-governmental sector, which are difficult to obtain from the department at the Ministry of Family, Labour and Social Policy. This is important, for example, in the context of the coordination and standardisation of the Ministry's cooperation programmes with non-governmental organisations for which the Ministry is required to implement the law. The establishment of the National Centre for Civil Society Development is intended to create legal, institutional and organisational conditions for the full and efficient use of public funds for the implementation of government civil society development programmes on the basis of openness, competitiveness and transparency, without compromising ministers' competence in the field of departmental programmes. This is a very important point, because we were accused of all the resources from the ministries going to this center. No, there will be absolutely no interference with what the ministries are doing. The draft law sets out the objectives, tasks and organisation of the national development centre and a new division of competences for the development of civil society, including public benefit and volunteering. The draft law also describes the special mode of commissioning public tasks in the centre, very transparent and much more organizational friendly. The intended effects of this regulation are horizontal coordination of policies to support the third sector, increased importance of the development of the third sector, improved communication in the development of the third sector, the creation of inter-ministerial institutional memory that accumulates experience and data currently in dispersal and which are the sole resources of individual ministries. The Centre Act provides for the transfer by the Minister for Social Security of regulatory powers in the field of public benefit and volunteering to the President of the Council of Ministers for the implementation of government grant and operational programmes for civil society of the Centre. The Civil Initiative Fund will therefore be implemented as the Centre's grant programme. The Act introduces clear selection criteria for projects in the competition procedure. Instead of the 26 criteria that currently operate in the Civil Initiative Fund, they will now be 4 and 7 in line with international standards, including sustainability criteria, so that the results of the project will also serve once it is completed. In addition, the Act introduces the principle that the most important criterion for the clearance of projects is the results achieved. The Act also introduces a mechanism for social control over the performance of public tasks. The final decision to grant the grant shall be adopted by a 7-person council in which two seats are reserved for representatives of the Council of Public Benefit Activities. Well, maybe that's it.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u47	Thank you. Please respond to the Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u48	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! If such farms are located in Natura 2000 areas or close to buildings, I must say that they are illegally located. In accordance with the law in force before the construction of the poultry farm, it is necessary to obtain a number of decisions. The first decision is an environmental decision. She has to be the first. An environmental decision must first be obtained before deciding on the conditions for the construction and development of the site. This environmental decision is made by the governor, the president or the mayor at the request of the investor. And now there are limitations. The limitations are that according to the Act on Access to Information, i.e. the so-called. The evaluation law requires environmental decisions for these farms. The first point is that in Natura 2000 areas, regardless of the size of the farm, it is necessary to carry out an environmental impact assessment. This environmental impact assessment shall be carried out and the decision shall be taken together with the Regional Environment Directorate. In addition, if it is a large poultry farm of more than 210 large conversion units or more than 52 500 chicken units, an environmental decision must also be obtained regardless of location and an environmental impact assessment must be carried out. In addition, an environmental impact assessment should be carried out for smaller farms, which are so-called. The second group, from 15,000 pieces to 52 499 exactly. It is also necessary to obtain a decision, but it is up to the governor to decide whether or not to draw up an environmental impact report. If it is near the nature reserve - a distance of 100 m, up to 100 m - if it is 100 m from residential areas, unbuilt urbanised, recreational and leisure areas, it is also necessary to obtain an environmental decision, while the governor decides whether an environmental impact report has to be drawn up. In addition, I would like to make it clear that making decisions on the conditions for the construction and development of the land for each investment belongs to the property of the governor, president or mayor of the city, i.e. it is not that according to law the investor decides himself. This is decided by the local government. It is difficult for me here to say how these procedures are carried out in individual local governments. Perhaps they are carried out unlawfully, which unfortunately happens sometimes. So that these rules that currently exist are quite restrictive provisions, one can say, and individual cases must be dealt with individually. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u57	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer the first question, I would like to say that these changes are a natural consequence of a decrease in the number of doctors who meet the requirements of the system doctor. This is a clear example, the reason for the decrease in the number of specialized teams. In recent years, the number of specialist teams has dropped from over 630 to 580. This is due to the fact that there are not enough doctors who are doctors of emergency medicine, and doctors who meet the requirements of the system doctor in accordance with the Act on State Medical Rescue. In view of this situation, we are forced to react. The first element that showed our response was the amendment of the regulation of 20 April 2016 on medical rescue operations, health services other than medical rescue operations, which may be provided by a medical lifeguard. This amendment was preceded by a very long consultation with the medical rescue community, but also with the nursing community. We must bear in mind that in the system, there are also more than 2,300 emergency nurses in medical rescue teams that meet the requirements of the Act. As far as this regulation is concerned, this scope of competence takes into account the skills of a medical lifeguard acquired during training. It is very important that we have clearly defined the path of training for medical rescuers from undergraduate studies, through six months of practice to the State exam, which will be carried out by the Centre for Medical Examinations, i.e. the rank of this exam is already very high. The range of operations that rescuers can perform, on the one hand, is due to their previous practice and the expansion of... We have to remember that 10 years ago, there were teams in the system without a doctor, which is basic teams. This number of teams is growing steadily. So that this regulation, which was published on 20 April, is the opposite of extending these powers. At the same time, we conclude the procedure of a similar regulation relating to the professional group of nurses and midwives. We are equalizing the privileges of emergency nurses for both the performance of operations and the administration of a much wider catalogue of medicines used in emergency care. This is all connected, of course, with the planned proposals for amendments to the Act on State Medical Rescue. We have now finished gathering comments on this bill. We are in the process of analyzing these comments carefully and we will be planning a concerted conference in order to convey the final shape of these proposals to parliamentary work. It should also be borne in mind that the gradual withdrawal of specialist teams is planned in the 2020 project, which is a relatively long vacathio legis, which will allow us to adapt our education even better. We have included in the bill additional courses that rescuers will have to take in order to acquire additional privileges. These are specialized courses and qualification courses, which correspond to the same courses for the professional group of nurses and midwives. Therefore, this investigation period for such a final version, where there will be no specialised teams, is extended. At the same time, during this consultation, we have a lot of voices from the community of emergency medicine doctors, from employers, that a lot of doctors want to continue working in the system. We analyze an additional solution, so-called. the rendez-vous system, which is a system that operates in many Western countries and the operation of which is that a team with a smaller composition, a double, i.e. without a doctor, i.e. a lifeguard driver and a doctor of the system, in certain special circumstances at the call of an ambulance arrives at the scene and provides appropriate assistance there, so this medical assistance is preserved. As far as the number of rescuers is concerned, there are 14 615 rescue teams working in the system, while - as I said - there are 2300 nurses here, so this number according to our estimates is absolutely enough to ensure the staffing of the medical rescue team. On the other hand, we are not concerned about the increased burden on the SORs, as the Act provides for mandatory hospital medical segregation at the level of hospital emergency services, [[♪ The bell ♪]] which will as if distribute patients requiring assistance at the level of the hospital emergency department and patients who will require assistance at the level of POZ or night Christmas aid. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u79	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to the Member's question, this is indeed the case, exactly what the structure of use and structure of broadband networks in Poland is like, as the Member put it in his question. In large urban centres, in medium-sized urban centres, where we deal with more wealthy customers of telecommunications services, these networks are built from private money telecommunications operators. The telecommunications market in Poland is extremely competitive and there, in these large, larger towns, these operators compete for customers, there is a high level of access to broadband, broadband Internet services. In Poland, we have an extremely strong cable TV industry and these cable TV operators in large, larger urban centres compete with our former national operator for broadband client. So when it comes to the state of saturation of broadband services of large, larger towns, it is absolutely satisfactory and it can be said that in these larger towns the free market and competition solves the problem itself, as well as the wealth of the portfolio, the wealth of the consumer of these services. The problem, however, is that the further we go into the area, the lower we go down in this structure of cities, towns, villages in Poland, the more these telecommunications investments become more expensive in those localities, and customers of telecommunications services cannot afford to pay such rates that would compensate telecom operators for the cost of building networks to serve them in those localities. For the Ministry of Digitalisation, improving broadband access is one of the most important issues that we deal with in the Ministry. We have the resources for this in the Operational Programme of Poland digital. I can say that, as part of the intervention we are planning at the moment, we have already announced a competition and in this competition we have set ourselves the goal of giving minimum investment obligations for telecom operators throughout Poland, that their obligation in the framework of the competitions for which we are allocating funds will be to connect more than 1,100 thousand. households. We're maxing out to cover, co-financing 3120 thousand. households throughout Poland. And it's like 66% households that have been identified by us as those that are intended to be connected under the grant scheme, are households located in locations less than 1 000. inhabitants and 82% of which these are households located in towns less than 5 000. residents. So thanks to this intervention we want to go to Poland, to which telecom entrepreneurs due to investment costs and costs of building broadband networks Don't let them get there. The second extremely important thing. Within the framework of this programme on connecting households, we also refer to the question of connecting schools to broadband Internet. This is what Minister Zalewska mentioned in her speech, referring to the Law of Education. And this is our great common program, which as the Ministry of Digitalization we carry out with the Minister of National Education. We picked each other... for $30,000. total schools in Poland, which operate in 19.5 thousand. address points, we have selected 10 200 school facilities, 10 200 address points, which are so far away from broadband nodes that telecom operators are not interested in building broadband access to these schools. And we also took these schools into this competition, dividing Poland into 79 competition areas. We precisely mean schools that are in the respective competition areas, and the obligation of entrepreneurs who will receive subsidies to connect households in this area will be to connect all educational establishments that do not have broadband access. So that we hope that thanks to this intervention that we are planning, we will be able to solve the problem of broadband connection to all educational establishments in Poland and significantly improve the access of residents, especially less populated areas, to the Internet.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u87	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I will begin my answer to the second question by referring to the topic related to subsidies. The allocation algorithm is based on the principle that the calculation of grants is based on the number of pupils attending schools and educational establishments run or subsidised by individual municipalities, powiats and self-government voivodships. In order to adjust the amount of subsidies to the educational tasks carried out by individual units of the local government, the algorithm depends on this level, among others. from the number of pupils and pupils and the structure of teachers' employment and take into account specific types and types of schools. The algorithm shall take into account the specificities of schools and institutions due to educational costs, e.g. vocational, sports, medical, artistic, youth education centres, special education, which is reflected in the additional importance applied to the students of the above mentioned school. institutions. Thanks to the application of such an extensive allocation algorithm, the amount of education subsidies charged to local government units remains in close relation to the costs of conducting educational tasks. Indeed, as you have pointed out, the grant is very detailed in terms of the zloty, while the subsidy, as I said, is charged with an algorithm. It is the income of local government units and it is the local government that gives the authorities, in the form of grants, the necessary money to conduct education policy and education. With regard to the basis of the curriculum and the way in which we are teaching, our proposed education reform is primarily intended to improve the quality of education at school so that the school is modern, so that it meets the needs of young people so that young people, i.e. students, can develop comprehensively. I think that this reform will end education only by answering test questions. We want the student to be able to evaluate certain phenomena on his own, we want to teach logical thinking. So this is such a very broad view of the student, who should finish a certain stage at school, in order to have some acquired skills, which will just be recorded in the programme basis during the particular final stages of education. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u110	Thank you, sir. Please respond to the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Interior and Administration of Minister Sebastian Gwałk. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u111	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Mr. Speaker! On 29 April 2015, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council [2] laying down the rules and general principles concerning mechanisms for control by Member States of the Commission's exercise of implementing powers. The Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration received a request from the Council of the City of Opole to change the borders of the city of Opole and the municipalities of Dobrzyń Wielki, Dąbrowa, Komprachcice, Prószków and Turawa from the district of Opole. Following preliminary analyses, including verification of the compliance of the information contained with the requirements of the Municipal Government Act of 8 March 1990, as well as the completeness of the attached documentation laid down in the Regulation of the Council of Ministers of 9 August 2001 on the procedure for the submission of applications for the creation, merger, division, abolition and establishment of the borders of municipalities, the granting of the status of a municipality or a town, the establishment and amendment of the names of the municipalities and their seats and the documents required in these cases, the proposal was qualified to submit to the Council of Ministers. The proposal proposes the following solution for joining Opola: from the municipality of Dąbrowa - Sławice and Wrzoski, parts of Karczów - together it is 1668 ha and 1426 inhabitants; from the municipality of Czarnowasy, Krzanowice, Świerkle, parts of Brzezie and Czyń Mały - together it represents more than 2753 ha and 4941 inhabitants; from Komprachcice - the village of Chmielowice and Żerkowice - together it is 532 ha, which also translates into 2115 inhabitants; from the municipality of Prószków - the village of Winów - it is over 278 ha and 694 inhabitants; the fifth element was a fragment, part of Zawada from the municipality of Turawa with an area of almost 88 ha and 233 inhabitants. In all the municipalities affected by the change, the inhabitants were negatively affected by the proposed solution. The authorities constituting these municipalities also expressed negative opinions. The inhabitants of Opole were positive about the proposed change. The Council of the City of Opole welcomed this change and also the Opole Voivode gave a positive opinion on this proposal. The Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration recommended this proposal for a positive examination by the Council of Ministers regarding the integration into the city of Opola of parts of the municipalities of Łódź Wielki, Dąbrowa, Prószków and Komprachcice. It did not suggest the Council of Ministers to include part of the municipality of Turawa. The integration of these new areas into the borders of the city of Opole in the Ministry's assessment will strengthen its potential and increase its competitiveness. The change will make full use of the existing potential in the region and contribute to the long-term development of the city and the Opole region. Using the surface, combining people's experience, knowledge, cultural values, involving new funds, will create good foundations for the development of the entire region, rural areas, small and larger cities. It is important, of course, that on 25 February 2016, the Council of the City of Opole adopted a resolution on expressing its willingness to accept a social contract as part of the extension of the administrative borders of the City of Opole. This contract declared the maintenance of educational establishments, including schools and kindergartens, the maintenance and modernisation of existing technical, municipal and historical infrastructure, the continuation of started investments in technical and municipal infrastructure, the maintenance and support of voluntary fire services activities, the maintenance and support of the activities of folk sports teams, the maintenance and support of cultural activities, support to non-governmental organisations, including support for the activities of organisations and associations involved in preserving and developing the cultural identity of the national minority, the transformation of the communities into district councils with budgets of 100,000. zł, support for entrepreneurs using the system of urban reliefs, maintenance and modernization of greenery and recreational areas, access to public communication by the Municipal Communication Centre in Opole, entry to the program of the Opolska Family Charter, of course, is an entrance to the city facilities for zloty. It should also be stressed that the decision to carry out changes in the borders of Opole was preceded by a meeting with all the local authorities concerned, where the parties had the opportunity to present their positions to the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The draft regulation of the Council of Ministers on the revision of the borders of the city of Opole on 12 July was the subject of work by the Systemic Affairs Team and the metropolitan and urban functional area team operating within the Joint Government and Local Government Commission. July 15th this year The President of the City of Opole and the President of the City Council of Opole sent a letter to the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration stating that a resolution on a specific proposal for financial support for the municipality of Zbędzyń Wielki will be presented to the City Council without delay. This support will concern the so-called. Janosik, payments for the equivalent part of the general subsidy for municipalities. In addition, this support will be increased by further financial resources necessary during the transitional period for the implementation of the tasks of the municipality and for the regulation of commitments. The details of the aid will be determined in the negotiations between representatives of local governments. Taking this into account, the Council of Ministers at its meeting of 19 July this year. took a positive decision on the integration into the city of Opola part of the communes of Zielona Wielki, Dąbrowa, Komprachcice and Prószków. In connection with the decision of the Council of Ministers, representatives of the city of Opola since September this year. They started discussions on financial issues with representatives of all the parties concerned. The purpose of the talks, first of all, was to establish procedures for matters resulting from changes in administrative borders, including the acquisition of assets, the agreement of the operating conditions of the budgetary units currently operating in the municipalities of Dąbrowa, Zbąń Wielki, Komprachcice and Prószków and the district of Opole. In addition, the identification of possible needs of municipalities of Dąbrowa, Łobień Wielki, Komprachcice and Prószków for additional funds in so-called. the transitional period, mainly in 2017, relating to the maintenance of the liquidity of budgets and the fulfilment of the standards for budgetary resolutions, notably in the context of the provisions of the Public Finance Act. Representatives of the city of Opola held a series of meetings with all interested local government units. These meetings resulted in the following arrangements. On 5 October 2016, a meeting took place at the City Hall of Opole, which was attended by the President of the City of Opola, the Treasurer of the City of Opola, the Mayor of the Municipality of Białyń Wielki and the Treasurer of the Municipality of Białyń Wielki. Wójt gmina Dobrzeń Wielki presented proposals on the financial needs of the municipality in order to ensure its smooth operation in 2017. It was agreed that the financial assistance that Opole would provide to the municipality of Dobrzeń Wielki in 2017 would amount to more than PLN 9.5 million. From recent information, we know that it will be almost PLN 10 million. On 13 October 2016, another meeting took place at the Opole City Hall. This meeting was set up, among others. with the treasurer of the municipality of Dąbrowa, that the support from the city's budget for the municipality in 2017 would amount to about PLN 3.6 million, of which 1800 thousand. PLN concerns the performance of own tasks and another 1800 thousand. PLN will relate to the repayment of liabilities held by the municipality. The preliminary analysis of the long-term financial forecast of the municipality of Dąbrowa and the data presented justify the guarantee for 2017 of support to that municipality in the amount of 1800 thousand. PLN for the performance of own tasks. The result of all these findings was, first, the resolution of the Opole City Council of 27 October 2016 on granting [[♪ The bell ♪]] the financial assistance to the municipality of Łobień Wielkie for the amount of 9 906 400 and the resolution of the City Council of Opole of 27 October 2016 on the allocation to the municipality of Dąbrowa of 1800 thousand. zł. Negotiations are currently underway on how these funds are to be transferred. Opole seems to have taken its responsibilities quite seriously, first of all, towards new residents, and also to the part of the municipalities that lose some income. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u245	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present a position towards the parliamentary draft law amending the Act on the Polish Air Navigation Agency, prints no 1047 and 1068. The amendment of the Act aims to ensure continuity of management of the activities of the Polish Air Navigation Agency. In the current situation, the procedure for entrusting the management of the agency's activities has been exhausted. To date, competition proceedings have not selected candidates that meet the requirements laid down in the Act. At the same time, a competition was launched for the position of President on the date of entry into force of the proposed law. Competitions initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Act shall be terminated. In addition, the project envisages a change in the requirements to be met by the candidate for appointment as President of the PAZP. The rapporteur of the committee just presented the amendments that were adopted at the committee's meeting, mainly concerning these requirements. The date of entry into force of the above Law is specified in the draft at the date following the date of publication. The need for such a short vacathio legis is due to the fact that the deadline for the duties of the President of the Polish Air Navigation Agency expires on 31 December 2016. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club strongly recommends amendments to this law, in print no 1047 and 1068. Thank you very much.

The first issue that the amendment regulates is all that is linked to the creation of a new efficient stream of quality grants. The second point or second point of the amendment is to improve the work related to the processing of applications for redemption in part or in whole student loans. This is also related to the matter of rebureaucratizing the functioning of higher education. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! As far as point one is concerned, that is, what is related to the creation of a new, effective stream of grants, it must be said that the existing grant system was primarily based on national leading scientific centres. The operation of national scientific centres was based on the establishment of these centres following competitions. I would like to point out that the existing Law on Higher Education indicates that the number of national scientific centres leading in particular fields of science cannot exceed three. Here I would like to point out that, as far as humanities are concerned, there are 13 disciplines in the field of humanities, nine disciplines in the field of social sciences, and 22 disciplines in the field of technical sciences. As a result, the system of competitions led to the fact that only a small proportion of individual disciplines could have their own representatives in the area of national scientific leading centres. The amendment proposed by the applicant, which is supported by the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice in its entirety, concerns the creation of a new factor identifying or pointing to scientific units, organisational units worthy of distinction and channelling towards them a stream of grants. This factor is the number of abiturients who have a 100% passed M.A. or a successful bilingual exam or abiturists who are finalists or winners of the national Olympics concerned or international Olympics. Well, these abitizens, choosing the different courses of study carried out by the organizational units, thus point to those places where the Ministry will place increased funds. So the factor is changing dramatically here, and it seems that as we move away from this stony, rigid system, we are moving towards decisions that will be carried out not directly by committees or ministry, but by the candidates themselves for higher education. The second point is the resignation, as I said, of grants for the financing of organisational units for the implementation of the improvement of the quality of education and the KRK. This is about those competitions that used to be held. The last competitions were held in 2012 and 2013, since then the competitions have not been announced, hence the resignation in the amendment of the operation of this grant. Finally, the amendment removes the Commission for Student Loans and Credits. The functioning of this committee in the current legal order has prolonged the process of remission. This was also due to the fact that the committee only met a few times a year. Amendment of the Law on Higher Education [[♪ The bell ♪]] together with two amendments that have already been adopted by the committee, will have full support from the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice. The second objective of the contested law is to improve the work related to the processing of applications for redemption in part or in whole student loans. This provision in the Act eliminates the Commission for Student Loans and Credits and transfers its powers to the competent minister. Over the years, the Commission has lost its rank and legitimacy. This change also makes the credit waiver procedure much shorter. During the work on the law in the Committee on Education, Science and Youth, many issues were raised, which also raise my doubts. Undoubtedly one of them is the lack of comprehensive consultation of the draft law with universities and listening to their voices. After all, it is their law that concerns the most, and because of the speed of proceding they have not had the opportunity to analyze the procedated content thoroughly. The second point of discussion is the award of quality grants only to state higher education institutions. As I understand the idea and the need to support the state sector, I am thinking about healthy competition between state and private universities. When both types of higher education exist in parallel in the higher education system, one of them, private, should not be discriminated against. This may undermine the constitutional principle of equality and, in my opinion, discrimination - after all, existing - very good private universities. The contested bill to improve the student loan waiver system is undoubtedly justified. We also look positively at the simplified system of funding pro-quality universities - although with some doubts, which I mentioned earlier. Hoping that the project will be further developed at a later stage of legislative work, the Free and Solidarni Circle proposes that the bill be further processed. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u303	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Since this is a second reading, I do not want to deal with your time with the details of the resolution at the moment, because at first reading I presented it quite precisely, and I think that most of you have also had the opportunity to read the details of the draft resolution proposed by the European Union's Committee on Affairs. I will concentrate on the work on this resolution in committee, as well as on the questions that have been asked in relation to the content of this resolution. It rained and returned during the first reading and during the committee meeting the question why this draft resolution at all, whether it was necessary or justified to raise this issue at this point. This is a really important question, so it is worth pointing out why the committee is proposing this draft resolution. To the committee on the basis of the so-called. the co-operation law affects a number of European Union acts to give an opinion. One such act which has led to an opinion is the draft regulation establishing the criteria and mechanisms for determining the Member State responsible for examining an application for international protection lodged in one of the Member States by a third-country national or a stateless person. The Commission is required to give an opinion on such a proposal and can also do so if it is a particularly important project, or is particularly controversial, in the formula for proposing a draft resolution to the entire High House. This may be a draft resolution on the fact that a project violates the principle of subsidiarity - and I would like to remind you that in mid-November this draft regulation was presented by Mrs Kloc, and was adopted by the High House - it may also refer in substance to the objectives of the proposed act. And just such a substantive reference is the proposed resolution that we are discussing today. So this project does not arise for a reason, it does not arise in a legal vacuum, it is not an incident or an idea of one of the Members - it is an initiative of the committee because it has influenced the opinion of the European Union legal act. What is this regulation, this proposal for a regulation of the European Union, which we particularly note in the draft resolution of the committee? Well, first of all, the so-called. permanent correction mechanism for the relocation of refugees. This is about introducing such a system, in short, which will make it possible that, if relatively more refugees are arriving in one selected Member State than other Member States, of course we know that such situations are taking place, then Member States do not accept refugees on their own initiative, but there will be a permanent mechanism which, as a rule, excludes the sovereignty of a Member State as to whether and how many refugees, in what amount, are to be accepted, automatically assigning these refugees to the State concerned. That's the first assumption. The second assumption is the possibility of excluding a Member State from such a mechanism - it is then charged with a fee, which would have to be quite frankly referred to as a penalty imposed on a Member State, with a penalty of not very little, because in the amount of 250 000. the euro for each unaccepted refugee. It was these two proposals that raised the greatest controversy among Members, and rightly so, not only among Polish Members. They are deeply controversial throughout Europe. To be clear, such proposals would violate the sovereignty of the Member States, not only of Poland, of each Member State of the European Union, if they were introduced, would exclude an important part of the refugee policy from the Member State's disposal. And imposing further penalties or sanctions because the State does not want to accept refugees on the basis of trust and automatic mechanism, that is, of course, an unprecedented issue, which is all the more unacceptable. And that's the essence of this draft resolution. In this draft resolution, we point out that these proposals cannot be accepted because they are against the sovereignty of a Member State and that they violate the principle of subsidiarity, which is very important in EU law. The committee therefore proposes that the Sejm react in the form of a resolution. The second question raised during the committee's work is whether this resolution does not deal with just one aspect, the opposition to the mechanisms proposed in the EU legislative act. It's true. I also explained it during my first reading. Since the resolution refers to a specific legal act, it does not necessarily discuss a number of other aspects, and there are not so few of them concerning refugee policy. But since, on the one hand, this allegation was repeated, and on the other hand, false arguments were raised that the Polish government was not even involved in humanitarian aid or financial support for third countries in the fight against the migration crisis, because no one was involved in the migration crisis of the eyes from the Polish side, and certainly not from the Polish government, closed, an amendment was tabled by Mrs Golbik to include this aspect too. This amendment was also welcomed by the representative of the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration at the committee meeting, so we decided as a committee to extend this resolution by such a paragraph, a paragraph saying that the government is not avoiding commitment, including financial commitment, to third countries in solving the causes of the migration crisis, because it is not repealed, which is best demonstrated by the fact that the financial amount allocated to the 2016 budget for humanitarian measures related to the resolution of the migration crisis is twice as high as in 2015, which is worth paying attention to in this discussion so that it is complete. In conclusion, it is appropriate that the government, in talks, in negotiations at European level, should know the voice of the Sejm and that this voice of the Sejm should also be a tool that the government can use in these negotiations. For this reason, it would also be good, and that is what I wanted to encourage the High House and your Members to make this voice as clear as possible. In this way it would become a clear, strong tool for the government, and yet that in the hands of the Polish government a refugee policy, which is an immanent element of the foreign policy of the state, lies, I hope, at the heart of all of us. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u335	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! This is another implementation of our electoral declarations. Since 1 January, we have eliminated the Ministry of Treasury. This is in line with previous announcements both in the election campaign and the announcement resulting from the exposé by Prime Minister Beata Szydło. However, we do not, of course, dispose of the wealth of the State Treasury. The role of such a specific coordinator of ownership policy will be played by the President of the Council of Ministers. The liquidation of the Ministry of Treasury is mainly due to the fact that we are leaving completely from privatisation processes. This ministry in its history was first established as the Ministry of Property Transformation treated as the Ministry of Privatization. Since it is not our job to privatise, to privatise the state's assets further, it is to multiply and take care of this property, so this ministry, which served as a privatization, has actually exhausted its capabilities and functions. Therefore, it is time for this ministry to be wound up. The proposed laws, both the Law on the Management of State Property and the Introductory Provisions, introduce a new formula for taking care of the State Treasury's assets. First of all, the most important provision of this law is ownership over companies of the State Treasury. It will be carried out by the President of the Council of Ministers, not directly, of course, but through a legal delegation - a regulation of the Council of Ministers delegates this management to individual industry ministers. Of course, individual industry ministers will be on the basis of a resolution of the Council of Ministers, i.e. such a delegation by the Prime Minister, to manage companies of the Treasury which will be in their field of interest. Besides, this process is already taking place this year, but it is only a process under the authority of the Minister of Treasury issued to individual industry ministers - such a package of energy companies is, among others. managed by the Minister of Energy. Another element of this law is the ordering and introduction of transparent staff policies into supervisory authorities and management bodies. Such a element of a transparent HR policy will be the creation of a Company Council with the participation of the State Treasury. The Council will be an advisory body providing comprehensive and professional support to the head of government in terms of coordination of ownership supervision, but also its extremely important task will be to evaluate candidates for corporate bodies with the participation of the State Treasury and state legal entities. We want delegated or selected persons to meet the highest quality standards. This is what this is about - raising the standards of ownership supervision when it comes to selection of staff. The Act also provides for a precise indication of the administrative bodies which will administer individual items of State property. It is also extremely important, you can say, to restore, change the principle and model of the disposal of shares and shares of the State Treasury. In general, there is a waiver of direct privatisation, while there are still a number of companies in which the State Treasury has little, it can be said, traces of shares. Therefore, these shares will be disposed of, but we want to return to the principle that this will be by virtue of a resolution of the Council of Ministers. This principle has worked for many years. Another element of such a comprehensive and orderly supervisory system will be the separation of a group of companies of major importance for the state economy. These are the most important, important companies - there will be dozens of them - in which the exercise of the powers of the State Treasury will be subject to special supervision by the Prime Minister, i.e. such a list of companies of importance for the state economy. The most important objective of managing the assets of the State Treasury and the expected result will be the long-term increase in the value of the national assets by the growth of the assets of individual companies with the participation of the State Treasury. The liquidation process of the Ministry of Treasury and the Law on Introductory Provisions provide for individual steps leading to the liquidation of the Ministry of Treasury. It is clear that such the most important element is the liquidation of the department itself: the Treasury, i.e. the rewrite and revision of many provisions in the Introductory Act, where the words of the State Treasury Minister and others will be replaced accordingly by other provisions. The liquidation of the Ministry of Treasury will also result in significant savings in administration. Of course, we provide for supervision, which on the one hand will be coordinated by the President of the Council of Ministers and, on the other hand, will be carried out, as has been the case so far, on the principles of delegation by individual Ministry Ministers, but the liquidation of the Ministry of Treasury itself will result in a significant reduction in administration and bureaucracy. We estimate that this can bring about PLN 100 million per year, so on a scale of 10 years, as it is defined, the amount, the financial impact of introducing individual laws is about PLN 1 billion. These are the most important provisions and the most important principles that we propose in the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property, as well as in the Law introducing the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u362	Thank you. Mr Ireneusz Gains, Frees and Solidarni have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u363	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Polish Free and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the Government's draft law on the rules for the management of state property and the Government's draft law on the introduction of the Law on the rules for the management of state property, 1053 and 1054 prints. As we can read in the explanatory memorandum, the Law on the Principles for the Management of State Property aims to improve the quality of the management of state property, including ownership supervision, by changing the functioning of the current model with significant structural defects. One of the main drawbacks of the current system is the exercise of rights in relation to State property, including shares belonging to the Treasury and public legal persons, by a large number of bodies and entities without organised coordination of state property management policy at the level of the Council of Ministers. It should be noted that issues related to the state wealth management were included not only in the Commercialisation and Privatisation Act, but also in more than 90 other laws and regulations, which made it difficult to develop a uniform, coherent approach to the State Treasury's ownership policy. In our view, therefore, it is important to appreciate the draft laws in question, as they contain a comprehensive regulation on the management of state property, formulated in a uniform and orderly manner. In terms of quality and systemic completeness of legislation, this is certainly a step in the right direction, albeit leaving a certain area as necessary for more precise regulation. As is usually the case with such extensive projects, and especially due to significant institutional changes, such as the liquidation of the Ministry of the Treasury, there are doubts, comments which should be discussed in substance during the work on the law in the parliamentary committees. I'll mention the two most important things in my assessment. First, Chapter 4 of the Law on State Property Management provides for the creation of a Company Council with the participation of the State Treasury and state legal persons. The Council is to be a consultative body providing comprehensive and professional support to the head of government when it comes to coordinating ownership supervision. In addition to the criteria laid down for members of supervisory boards, there are no additional requirements which would guarantee the professionalism and experience of the members of the board. It is difficult to regard as such the vague, discretionary term: • it has the knowledge, skills and experience appropriate to perform its duties in the Council and to carry out its duties, and gives the guarantee of the proper performance of those duties. It must not be allowed, Mr Minister, ladies and gentlemen, to have incompetent persons in the body advising the Prime Minister of the Government of the Republic of Poland on such an important matter as ownership control over the state estate. It is necessary to apply higher minimum criteria in this case, e.g. the criterion of 5 years of experience in working on a specific job or in performing a specific function in this area or other more specific criteria. Secondly, the content of the Act does not indicate whether the government will complete the privatisation processes started or whether the so-called sales will take place. the remaining shares completely redundant for the State Treasury, because - as Dr. Grażyna Majcher-Magdziak, Minister for Treasury Companies and privatisation in the so-called. the business shadow office of the Business Centre Club, a member of the Social Dialogue Council, was not planned to create any specialised entity or agency that would assume the task of disposing of these assets from the liquidation of the Ministry of Treasury. While the procedure for the sale of shares of state-owned companies is foreseen, it has been more bureaucratic than before and for any such transaction it is necessary to obtain the approval of the Council of Ministers. This may make it more difficult to manage state assets, because in the hands of various ministries of industry that carry out ownership functions outside large-scale, economically important companies, there will be insignificant holdings of shares that will have to be taken care of, and this needs to be increased costs. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u395	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! I have the honour to present to you a draft law on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, a project which uses the experience that has been accumulated during the ten-year functioning of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the General Treasury. The basic changes envisaged by the project are the extension of the powers of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the State Treasury. This extension is first manifested by an increase in the scope of the subjective replacement provided by the Attorney General, this time already the Republic of Poland. This means that, in addition to the bodies representing the State Treasury, the Public Prosecutor's Office will be able to replace the state authorities in administrative proceedings, as well as in certain situations public legal persons or persons with the State Treasury or public legal persons. We trust that this extension of the powers of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the State Treasury will be effective and will lead to the implementation of the costs incurred by those entities that I have just mentioned. Further powers entrusted to the President of the Public Prosecutor's Office relate, first, to the establishment of an arbitration court at the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland, in which entities with the State Treasury will be able to settle their disputes, without burdening them on the judiciary or economic arbitrage, and also disputes which sometimes occur between the organisational units of the State Treasury, are disputes that can never be brought to the courts because the organisational units of the State Treasury do not have legal personality, but these disputes are real. Finally, the scope of the opinion of the Public Prosecutor's Office of the State Treasury is increased. Opinioning activities will in principle be optional, except for two types of situations: firstly, all contracts, all legal activities concluded by state units, if they are above PLN 100 million, will have to be assessed by the Public Prosecutor-General, secondly, legal advice contracts exceeding the amount of 500 000. PLN per year will also have to be evaluated by the Public Prosecutor's Office of the State Treasury... already by the Public Prosecutor's Office of the Republic of Poland. Very often, as the reports of the Supreme Audit Chamber pointed out, no arrangements were made from privatisation agreements, and mostly no non-financial arrangements for privatisation agreements were carried out. All of this led us to equip the Attorney General with new tools that would allow us to control the various kinds of actions that are taking place in the Treasury companies, and this will also be the instrument of the Prime Minister who will control it, not the tool of the Supreme Auditorium, which states that something was wrong, even directs applications to the prosecution, but then it is too late, because it is often impossible to recover or stop processes that have already gone through. It will be a specialized entity that will oversee the projects of various types of ownership changes, contracts and will also fulfil the previous tasks of the procedural replacement, but extended to replace or represent the various entities of the Treasury. The problem of representation of the state, so-called The fiscal, civil-law trade and the protection of public property interests are socio-economically important issues, it is complex in terms of jurisdiction and, at the same time, controversial in doctrine, because it is at the interface of two areas, two regulatory methods: public law and private law. For a long time, attempts have been made to regulate this issue, since already in the Middle Ages, Mr S.A., there was a prosecutor acting as an adviser and a substitute for the royal crown and the State Treasury, and in the 19th century in some countries there was a significant development of prosecutors' institutions and prosecutors in a manner that suited the organisation and needs of the modern state. In fact, in all modern countries, the question of the management of the State Treasury's assets and the representation of the state in private trade is of interest to legislators who adopt different models to regulate this issue, seeking a certain balance in the regulatory field and effective protection. On the basis of the systems of European countries, three methods of regulation can be distinguished - it is a pity that there is no time - i.e. British, German and system adopted in Poland, adopted from the system of German law, based on civilistic structures of representation. [[Thief.]] In the Republic of Poland, the matter of the protection and representation of the state's assets became particularly important with the moment of departure from the planned economy and the transition to the market economy system. The representation system of the state and the management of the State's assets and the institution governing the Treasury, the representation of the State Treasury were regulated in the Act of 8 July 2005 on the Public Prosecutor's Office of the General Treasury. I will only mention that 15 years have been worked on this law in Poland for various reasons and reasons, but I think that the solutions adopted in this law were not bad. During the interwar period - this was also referred to by my previous speaker - from 1918 until 1954 the Public Prosecutor General functioned in Poland and effectively defended the estate of the State Treasury. I refer to the statements, reflections of Bukowecki's then president, who said why this could happen and why it was happening. Is it now time to judge and review these ways of representation? The idea is to create an attractive and cost-effective one, let us stress: cost-effective, alternative to burdening in the case of disputes between entities controlled by the state of justice, and to charge the budgets of these persons with substitution costs, the cost of legal services, which are currently largely transferred outside. And it is not hypothetical that the provision of legal services is a way of delivering the company's money. These are not hypothetical cases, these are not cases that happen occasionally. Moreover, the cases of foaming between companies controlled by the State Treasury are not hypothetical either. There are cases where two companies from the same group correspond to each other by means of a network firm. It's pathology, it's just pathology. So, I hope I have referred to all the doubts you have raised. I hope that I will have the opportunity to address other doubts, if necessary, in case of referral to committee. Thank you very much.
