ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u429	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, It has been my honour to present to the High House a draft law on the rules implementing the Law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal and on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. Two laws: on the status of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and, the second, on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal introduce a new system of law on the Constitutional Tribunal. These laws are intended to replace the existing rules on matters relating to the tribunal. In order to relieve these laws from the provisions amending, transitional and adapting, as well as to ensure consistency and transparency of the new legislation, the group of Members proposed to include provisions on the entry into force, amending, repealing, transitional and adapting the introductory provisions in a separate law. In order to ensure the transparency of the act, it was divided into four chapters. The systematics of the Act is in line with the requirements of the principles of legislative technique, except that, for reasons of clarity of the act, a provision repealing the Constitutional Court Act of 2016 was made before the amending provisions. Such a technique has already been applied in legislative practice, among others. in the Law of 30 August 2002. Provisions introducing the Law on the System of Administrative Courts and the Law on the Procedure of Administrative Courts. At that time, there was no doubt about it. The draft provides that the Law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal shall enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. They shall enter into force on the day following the date of their notification and Article. This Regulation shall enter into force on 1 January 2018 and shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official Journal of the European Union. Prior entry into force of Article. It is necessary to define, among other things, the internal organs of the Constitutional Tribunal and what is the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and its competence. In the case of Article. The proposal provides that these provisions will enter into force on 1 January 2018 on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal. In this way, the projector gives time for activities related to the liquidation of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal and the establishment of the Registry of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Office of the Legal Service of the Constitutional Tribunal. The Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of Article. Article 2 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013. 16 and 17, Art. 19, Art. 31 and Article 31 35, which shall enter into force on the day following the day of the notice. This is intended to enable the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to be paid the basis for the remuneration and the remuneration of judges of the Constitutional Tribunal at rest, as well as to ensure that the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal take office at the Constitutional Court, as the said provisions determine the beginning of office. The rules are also intended to enable certain judges to retire. The draft implementing provisions repeal the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 22 July 2016 but temporarily maintain the provisions of the Article. Article 18 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 On the basis of the fact that the liquidation process of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal is spread over time and will not be completed until 1 January 2018, when the Registry of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Office of the Legal Service of the Constitutional Tribunal are established. The introductory law contains amending provisions. This is a consequence of the fact that the laws on the status of judges of the tribunal and on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the tribunal affect the content of other laws. The proposed amending provisions are intended to ensure the uniformity and consistency of the legal system. In cases initiated and not completed before the date of entry into force of the Organisation and Procedure Act before the Court of Justice, proceedings shall be conducted on the basis of the principles laid down in that Law. The proceedings before the Court of Justice before the entry into force of the Act on organisation and procedure before the Court of Justice shall remain in force. This ensures that the interests in progress are protected. The draft law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Court of Justice does not change the rules of procedure in a diametric manner, but arranges them and complements them with solutions which have so far been adopted in lower rank acts, although according to the case-law of the Court of Justice they should be included in the law. The Act on the Status of Judges of the Tribunal shall significantly redefine certain duties of Judges of the Tribunal. As this represents a significant change in the number of civil rights of the judge (e.g. right to privacy - disclosure of assets, in this context This should be seen) compared to the moment when the current judges decided to accept the choice, the projector allows them to re-verify the decision. The draft law proposes that the judge of the Tribunal whose term of office began before the date of entry into force of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal may, within one month of the date of its entry into force, make a statement to the President of the Tribunal that, in view of the introduction during his term of office of office of the new rules for the performance of the duties of the Judge of the Tribunal as set out in Article. Article 11 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 3, Article 13 and Article 13 of Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 February 2011 laying down the rules and general principles concerning It is simply a judge's decision - if he is not embarrassed by the fact that he is to disclose a declaration of property, he does not have to make such decisions. The Court of Justice shall be retiring in this case on the first day of the month following the month in which the declaration is made. The President of the Tribunal shall declare the transition to rest by way of order. The designer assumed that the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal would be operational until 31 December 2017. On 1 January 2018, it will be abolished and the Constitutional Tribunal and the Office of the Legal Service of the Constitutional Tribunal will be established. Until 31 December 2017, the Tribunal's office is expected to operate on the basis of most of the existing rules. From the day following the date of publication of this Act, only the head of the Tribunal's office will be wound up. Its duties shall be taken over by the President of the Tribunal or by a person authorised by him. There will be a position of the person responsible for the liquidation of the office and the establishment of new cells. During the transitional period, the staff of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal shall retain their rights of employment and carry out their duties in accordance with the scope of the existing acts on the basis of which their employment relationship was established. In the period up to 30 September 2017, workers will be presented with new working conditions and wages. If they refuse, their employment relationship will expire on 31 December 2017. The same applies to workers who will not be offered new conditions. This process is based on appropriate procedures. Departure workers will be guaranteed adequate checks in accordance with the Law on special rules for the termination of employment relationships with workers on grounds not relating to workers. The proposed solution therefore meets the standards for the protection of workers' rights. Changes in the structure of the organisational units of the tribunal are necessary. They are intended to improve the functioning of the tribunal and optimise its organisational and administrative activities and ensure that the subsidiary of the tribunal is of a professional nature. The creation of a rational organisational structure of the subsidiary bodies of the Court will encourage savings and reduce the annual maintenance costs of the Constitutional Tribunal's office. The reorganisation process will be spread over time for two reasons. Firstly, the designer wants to provide employees with all statutory employee guarantees. Secondly, for reasons of public finances, the projector wanted to avoid a situation in which it would be necessary to amend the budget law during the financial year due to the new organisational structure of the subsidiary bodies of the Court. The new structure was not included in the current financial plans of the Court, as at the time of their establishment the draft law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Court was not known. Therefore, the adaptation provisions require that when planning the budget of the Court for 2018, the Registry of the Court and the Office of the Legal Service of the Court, which will be operational from 1 January 2018, be taken into account. Earlier acts will be issued specifying the organisational structure and the number of employees to be employed in those units. This is intended to facilitate the formation of the organisation and staff resources of the law firm and the Legal Service Office. In view of the need to ensure the proper functioning of the Court and to enable changes to be made to the organisational structures of its subsidiary bodies, the draft provides for certain internal acts, such as the rules of procedure of the Court, the Statute of the Court's Registry, the Statute of the Court's Office of Legal Service, the Ethics Code of the Tribunal. It should be stressed that the statutes of the Court and the Office of the Legal Service are to be adopted before new subsidiary bodies are established. This will make it possible to define employment policies and prepare a fair draft budget for the Court. The creation of new units is also intended to be carried out by the President of the Court by 31 August 2017 of regulations on specific issues relating to the number, the list of posts in the organisational units concerned, the qualification requirements for the occupation of these posts, the detailed scope of tasks in these positions and the manner and conditions for their execution, as well as the rules for the remuneration of the staff of the Registry and the Office of the Legal Service. Acts concerning the Registry of the Court and the Office of the Legal Service of the Court are to enter into force on 1 January 2018, i.e. when these entities were set up. This is intended to prevent the existence of a double legal regime concerning the organisational structures of the tribunal. The laws on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Tribunal and on the status of judges of the Tribunal, which introduces the proposed law, shall be adopted during the period when the term of office of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal expires. At the same time, the public is informed of a number of actions of the Constitutional Tribunal which do not comply with the Constitutional Tribunal Act in force. They consist, first, of preventing the three judges of the Tribunal elected by the Sejm for the 8th term of office from attending general meetings during which a number of relevant decisions are taken, secondly, the issuing of a legally defective ruling on the signature of K 44/ on 7 November 2016 by the Constitutional Tribunal. 16, in which the judges considered the rules governing the election of candidates as President of the Constitutional Tribunal to be constitutionally compatible. The disadvantages of this settlement consist, first, of an unlawful change in the composition of the Constitutional Tribunal ruling contrary to the literal requirement of the Constitutional Tribunal Act, secondly, in the statement of reasons, i.e. in the non-normative part of the judgment, granting the general assembly the power to modify the Constitutional Tribunal Act and, thirdly, to take action to issue contrary to the Constitutional Tribunal's Rules of Procedure. Cumulation of the circumstances of, first, the changes in the legal system, second, the expiry of the term of office of the President of the Tribunal, third, the actions taken by the Tribunal and its President, which do not comply with the Constitution and the Constitutional Tribunal Act, may make it impossible to effectively elect candidates for the position of President of the Tribunal or appoint a President within a certain period of time. In the event of such an emergency, the drafter proposed the introduction of an adjustment body, namely the position of the judge acting as President of the Tribunal. The proposed solution is exceptional, temporary and is dictated by exceptional circumstances. The drafter charged the President of the Republic of Poland with the obligation to entrust such a judge with the duties of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal. It justifies this as follows. First of all, the president, based on the article. 126 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland has the task of upholding the Constitution. This task also concerns ensuring the sound and efficient functioning of public institutions through the cooperation of the authorities, as set out in the preamble to the Constitution. Secondly, the legislator authorised the President of the Republic of Poland to introduce a state of emergency at the request of the Council of Ministers. in the event of a threat to the constitutional system of the State, Article. Point (b) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland - which undoubtedly shows that in critical moments the President is obliged to act to ensure the stability of the state. Third, granting the President of the Republic of Poland the power to designate a person acting as President of the Constitutional Tribunal is justified by the fact that the Constitution of the Republic of Poland has entrusted the President with the power to appoint the President of the Constitutional Tribunal from among the candidates designated by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. This power is the prerogative of the President. If there were circumstances that would prevent the President from legally setting up the President of the Tribunal in December 2016, it would be appropriate for the President to appoint a person acting as President who will carry out a procedure within the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal to appoint candidates for the position of President in a manner consistent with the rules of law. The President will appoint such a judge with a resolution. For legal reasons, this provision requires countersignation. The main task of the judge acting as President is to hold a lawful general assembly which will appoint candidates for the position of President and present them to the President of the Republic of Poland. It will also ensure the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. Only the judge of the Tribunal may be a person acting as President. The President shall elect a grandstand judge who shall be entrusted with the duties of President from among the governors with the longest term of office in the general court or at the central state administration in positions relating to the application of the law. This standard is intended to guarantee that the President's action will not be arbitrary and that he will elect a judge who, due to his professional experience, including his organisational work, will give the guarantee of proper management of the institution during extraordinary circumstances and will ensure a smooth and reliable restoration of the legality of the tribunal. Such a person shall carry out his or her duties only until the President of the Tribunal has been appointed. The law provides for 1 month to carry out the selection procedure. This is a rational term which will provide sufficient time for selection and will not constitute abuse of the law by excessive exercise of powers by a person who is not the President of the Tribunal. The proposed solution is to implement the balance of power and to fulfil the constitutional function that it is the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal that presents candidates for the position of President of the Constitutional Tribunal to the President. It would therefore be unacceptable to entrust matters relating to the functioning of the tribunal to someone outside the judicial body of the tribunal. The temporary assignment of the duties of the judge to the President of the Tribunal does not make that person President. It is only a person who is to organise and conduct an appropriate, lawful procedure for the presentation of candidates for President at the General Assembly. The President must appoint the President of the Tribunal from among the candidates designated by the General Assembly and this constitutional standard is to be maintained. The person acting as President shall ensure that the procedure is lawful. The draft confers powers on the judge exercising the duties of the President of the Tribunal, which will enable him to direct the tribunal during a transitional period and ensure that the situation at the Constitutional Court is brought to an effective level in accordance with the Constitution and the laws. It will prevent the tribunal from being paralyzed. The appointment of a judge acting as President shall not be compulsory. It will only occur in an emergency situation, defined by the grounds of the Act. These are cases where, until the date of publication of this law, the General Assembly, firstly, was not convened by the President of the Tribunal, secondly, it was convened by the President contrary to the requirements of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 2016, because it was in force, thirdly, did not submit candidates for the position of President of the Tribunal to the President of the Tribunal, fourthly, submitted candidates for the position of President of the Tribunal, and the President did not appoint the President of the Tribunal, fifthly having appointed candidates for the position of President of the Tribunal in breach of the provisions of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 2016. The procedure in this respect is laid down in the draft Introductory Provisions Act. The mechanism set out in the Introductory Provisions is the accident power of the General Assembly to present the candidates for President and the powers of the President to appoint that body. The procedure guarantees representativeness of the choice, since it assumes that at least one of the candidates must always be supported by at least five members of the General Assembly. Nor does it restrict the constitutional powers of the President. The voting procedure by the general assembly for individual candidates is also the legal form of this assembly by means of a resolution, the purpose of which is to present candidates for the position of President of the Republic of Poland. In view of the exceptional circumstances, the proposed mechanism is more effective in expressing the joint opinion of the Authority and respecting the preferences of the various members of the General Assembly. In the face of a dispute with the Constitutional Tribunal, the proposed solution gives the opportunity to reach a broad consensus on the persons to be presented to the President. In addition, the mechanism does not differentiate between the powers or powers of the members of the General Assembly, and each member of the General Assembly has an impact on the final list of candidates for the position of President of the Constitutional Tribunal, regardless of what position he takes in the dispute over the Tribunal. In this way, equality shall be ensured for judges in the exercise of their office and the equal weight and individual nature of the votes cast in the procedure referred to in Article. Paragraph 194. 2 constitutions. One vote shall ensure the representativeness of the candidates and, in view of the fact that neither the constitution nor the law determines how many candidates are to be submitted to the President, it should be assumed that each of the judges of the Tribunal may indicate the preferences for his candidate as President. This confirms that the General Assembly shall, in the form of a resolution, present as candidates a certain number of votes. The proposed mechanism is intended to allow for a quick selection of candidates for the position of President and thus lead to the implementation of the standard resulting from the Article. Paragraph 194. 2 constitutions. In view of the fact that it is referred to in Article. 21 of the draft procedure is different from that indicated in the Act on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Court, which cannot be extended to other circumstances. It may only be applied in the case to which the introductory provisions apply. The implementing act is to enter into force 14 days after the date of publication, with the exception of the provisions concerning the entry into force of the main laws, the repeal of the Constitutional Tribunal Act of 2016, the structure of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal, the institution acting as President of the Tribunal and its powers, and the provisions on the election of candidates for President by the General Assembly. Those provisions are to enter into force on the day following the date of publication. The special circumstances connected with the dispute around the tribunal and the need to protect the legitimate operation of public authorities in a democratic legal state result in the loss of force of the existing Constitutional Tribunal Act, which is the majority of its provisions, being before the entry into force of the Act on the Status of Judges of the Tribunal and the Act on the organisation and procedure of the Constitutional Tribunal. The legislator introduces a completely new legal system governing the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal. The 14-day period during which there will be no complete regulation concerning the Constitutional Tribunal, because only certain provisions of the 2016 Act on the status of judges of the Tribunal, the Law on the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Tribunal and the Introductory Act will be in force, are to be able to adapt to the new legal situation without having to take action on the basis of an act which is about to expire. This is also to prevent a collision between the former law and the implementing rules. At the same time, it should be stressed that, within 14 days of the publication of the Act, the implementing provisions, despite the lack of overall regulation, which guarantee the continuity of the functioning of the body and its organisational units, will be maintained and the powers of the judges of the Constitutional Tribunal and of the employees will not be infringed. The proposed solution is intended to enable the situation in the Court of Justice of its activities to be settled and to bring the organisation and procedure of proceedings before the Constitutional Tribunal into legal order. [[Chairman of the session includes Deputy Marshal Ryszard Terlecki of the Sejm]] We proceed with the examination of item 42. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and some other laws (print No 1048). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Mr Łukasz Piebiak to present the justification for the bill. You're welcome, Minister.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u521	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! This time something absolutely seems uncontroversial. This draft law aims to ensure the effective implementation of Regulation No 655/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council establishing a European Account Preservation Order procedure to facilitate cross-border recovery of claims in civil and commercial matters. That Regulation shall apply from 18 January 2017. The EU Regulation aims to make it easier for creditors to execute their claims by obtaining collateral in the debtor's bank account irrespective of the Member State in which the account is held and under the same conditions in all Member States. So-called. The European Account Preservation Order will provide an alternative to the equivalent national account collateral measures and its additional advantage will be that the order issued in one Member State will be automatically recognised and enforceable in the other Member States. In addition, it will be a significant facilitation for creditors to obtain information on the debtor's bank account in certain situations for the purpose of issuing a European Preservation Order in such an account. The regulation obliges Member States to create an effective mechanism to facilitate access to such information. As is well known, European Union regulations are directly applicable in the Member States and generally do not require transposition. However, certain provisions of this Regulation need to be developed and supplemented in Polish law. Therefore, the project prepared by the Ministry of Justice and presented today introduces amendments primarily to the Code of Civil Procedure. In particular, these changes consist in: firstly, of the conclusion that, in the field not regulated by the Regulation and in the provisions added to it, c.p.c. the European Account Preservation Order procedures shall apply, secondly, to the determination of the jurisdiction of the court in matters for issuing a European Account Preservation Order in Poland, thirdly, to the determination of the authorities competent in Poland to take different actions in proceedings concerning a European Account Preservation Order, including the indication that the Bank Account Information Authority will be the Minister of Justice in Poland, fourthly, to determine the type of appeal available from the court's decision to issue a European Bank Account Preservation Order and to rule on the challenge of other decisions taken as a result of the recognition of the protection measures provided for in the Regulation and fifth and lastly, to indicate that the European Bank Account Preservation Order originating in a court of another Member State constitutes in Poland the title of protection on the basis of which enforcement proceedings may be initiated without the need to obtain exequatur. The amendments to the law on bailiffs and executions, which this draft also introduces, essentially extend the scope of the provisions on bailiff activities in enforcement proceedings and safeguard proceedings for the execution of European freezing orders. In turn, the amendments provided for in the Banking Law and the Act on cooperative savings and credit banks provide for access to information of banking secrecy to the Minister of Justice in so far as it is necessary to carry out the tasks of information authorities in accordance with the Regulation and specify the procedure in which the Minister will obtain that information. Information will be obtained from central information about accounts in the form of so-called. aggregate information which may also include information on non-natural holders. According to the draft law, the law should enter into force on 18 January 2017, i.e. on the date on which the Union Regulation will apply. It seems that this term is available, so without any delay we will do what we have to do. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u522	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Bartłomiej Wróblewski will be the first to speak on behalf of the Law and Justice Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u523	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice a position on the government draft law amending the Civil Procedure Code Act and certain other laws, together with the draft implementing act, print No 1048. The aim pursued by the Council of Ministers in the preparation of the project must be to ensure the effective implementation of Regulation No 655/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 15 May 2014 laying down the procedure for a European Account Preservation Order in order to facilitate cross-border recovery of claims in civil and commercial matters. This regulation is intended to enable creditors to obtain and secure their claims in the debtor's bank account under the same conditions in all Member States, regardless of where the account is held. As you know, according to the article. In principle, the 288 Treaty on the Functioning of the European Union applies directly to the Member States. In this case, however, we share the view that it is necessary to amend certain provisions of Polish law, as well as to introduce new regulations, without which proper and effective implementation of the regulation will not be possible. Therefore, we consider it justified to amend and supplement the Code of Civil Procedure, and also in the Bank Law, the Law on cooperative savings and credit registers, the Law on bailiffs and executions and the Law on judicial costs in civil matters. We agree that the bill under consideration will facilitate the creditor's recovery of claims by creating an additional instrument to safeguard claims which are important in cross-border cases in particular, while the debtor will impede his escape from execution while ensuring that his interests are protected. The terms of the proposed regulation in the Code of Civil Procedure are provisions on the issuing by the Polish court of a European order to secure a bank account and execute such an order in Poland. They argue that the Ministry of Justice is obliged to provide information on the debtor's bank accounts located in Poland. Nor does it raise any objections to entrusting bailiffs with the execution of the European Account Preservation Order. Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, I declare that the club will support further work on the bill. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u530	Thank you. Mr Krzysztof Paszyk will now speak on behalf of the Polish People's Party Club.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u531	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! I have the honour on behalf of the Polish People's Party Parliamentary Club to present our position on the government draft law amending the Code of Civil Procedure Act and some other laws. This proposal aims to ensure the effective implementation of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union establishing a European Account Preservation Order procedure to facilitate cross-border recovery of claims in civil and commercial matters. The Regulation entered into force in July 2014 and is to apply from 18 January next year. Although regulations of the European Union are in principle directly applicable in the Member States, it is necessary to amend certain provisions of Polish law and introduce new regulations, without which proper implementation of the Regulation would be impossible. The matter affected by the regulation is so broad that it is necessary to amend the Civil Procedure Code. According to the draft, the creditor will be able to secure his claims in the debtor's bank account under the same conditions irrespective of the State in which the account is held. European Account Preservation Orders issued in one Member State will be automatically recognised and enforced in another Member State without having to initiate a separate procedure. Under Polish law, this applies not only to bank accounts but also to accounts maintained by cooperative savings and credit banks, as has already been mentioned. On the other hand, the execution of such safeguards will take place on the basis of the rules adopted in the Member State for similar measures - in Poland it is the seizure of a bank account - with changes resulting from the Regulation. The creditor will be able to obtain an order before the opening of legal proceedings, as well as at any stage of the proceedings until the judgment or approval or the conclusion of a judicial settlement has been given, and after the creditor has obtained a decision requiring the debtor to pay the creditor's claim. A significant facilitation for creditors is the possibility to obtain information on the debtor's bank accounts for the purpose of issuing a European Preservation Order in such an account. The Regulation obliges Member States to create a mechanism to facilitate access to information on the debtor's bank account. The implementation of these solutions into Polish law will certainly facilitate citizens and companies, including particularly small and medium-sized ones, the investigation of cross-border claims and the effectiveness of enforcement of judgments in civil and commercial matters concerning cross-border disputes, which should reduce the risk of commercial transactions. This will also increase the payment discipline of debtors. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Therefore, on behalf of the Parliamentary Club of the Polish People's Party, I wanted to request that the project be directed to further work in committee. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u543	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I would like to provide a justification for the examination by the High Chamber of Government of the draft law amending the Law on Hunting Law and amending the Act amending the Law on Hunting Law, print no. 1042. The main objective of a draft law submitted by the Council of Ministers on amending the Law on Hunting Law and amending the Law amending the Law on Hunting Law is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 10 July 2014, marked with a sign. P 19/13. In the judgment cited above, the Constitutional Tribunal found that the Article was incompatible with the Constitution. Article 27 (2) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 Law Hunting Law, because this provision entitles the property to be covered by the regime of the hunting circuit, without providing adequate legal means to protect the rights of the owner of the property. It must be stressed that the Constitutional Tribunal has not questioned either the model adopted for the hunting economy, nor the requirements for hunting circuits, nor the possibility of covering the property with a hunting circuit without the consent of its owner. The execution of the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal is aimed at: a new procedure for the division into hunting circuits, taking into account the participation of owners and users of perpetual properties in the opinion of the draft resolution on division; the introduction of the possibility of a judicial ban on hunting because of religious beliefs or moral principles; the introduction of the possibility of redress where the previous use of the property has been substantially limited or completely excluded from the hunting circuit; the imposition of information obligations in respect of collective hunting. These solutions significantly strengthen the powers of real estate belonging to the hunting circuit and reduce the disadvantages associated with their obligation to carry out hunting by the tenants or managers of the hunting circuit, while maintaining the current model of hunting and the principles of creating hunting circuits that provide the conditions for hunting. In addition, the project aims at adapting certain statutory delegations to the requirements laid down in Article. 92. 1 Constitution of the Republic of Poland. According to Art. 92. 1 The Constitution of the Republic of Poland of the Regulation shall be issued by the bodies indicated in the Constitution, on the basis of the detailed authorisation provided for in the Act and for its implementation. This means that the statutory regulation must not be of a blank character, i.e. leave to the authority itself the possibility to regulate the whole complex of issues on which there is no direct regulation in the text of the Act. In order to avoid the allegation of the bleaching of the laws, it is proposed to regulate in the law the issues so far regulated by regulations, such as the rules for drawing up and amending annual hunting plans and multiannual hunting plans. In addition, the draft provides for the adaptation of certain statutory delegations to the constitutional model for guidelines for the adoption of implementing acts. Finally, the proposed bill aims to introduce mechanisms to strengthen the supervision of the Minister responsible for environmental affairs of the Polish Łowiecki Union and the disciplinary tenants and managers of hunting circuits in the implementation of annual hunting plans. In this respect, it was considered appropriate to align the situation of the tenants and managers of the hunting circuit and to extend the obligation to contribute to the costs of protecting the forest from game to the managers of the hunting circuit. Given that the non-executive or lack of hunting plans, equivalent to the inability to conduct a hunting economy, may lead to an excessive increase in the population of wild game threatening the agricultural and forestry economy and, in the event of increasing migration to areas inhabited by people, also the safety of persons and property, it is also proposed to introduce the possibility to terminate the agreement to lease the hunting circuit or to repeal accordingly the decision excluding the hunting circuit from leasing and transferring it to the Management Board in the event of non-execution by tenants or managers of hunting plans. Moreover, given that the Polish Hunting Union is not an association but a compulsory association, similar to professional self-government, which performs public-law tasks, including the disposal of wild animals that constitute a national good, it is proposed, among others.: imposing an obligation on the Polish Union of Hunters to submit annual information to the Minister responsible for the environment, as well as extending the proposed regulations on supervision of the right to request resolutions and explanations, the possibility of imposing a fine and the possibility to apply to the court for the repeal of the illegal or statutory resolution of the Polish Union of Hunting. The bill also provides for changes in the scope of judicial review in matters of acquisition and loss of membership in the Polish Łowiecki Union, the loss of membership in the hunting circle and the imposition of disciplinary penalties on a member of the Polish Łowiecki Union. These changes are due to differences in case law that have arisen under the existing law. Finally, I would like to stress that although the main objective of the proposed amendment to the Hunting Law is to implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal on the proper protection of landowners' rights in the Hunting District, some of the proposed solutions will undoubtedly be helpful in the fight against African swine fever, ASF. In particular, indicate here are the following: firstly, the indication as one of the grounds for amending the annual hunting plan and the multi-annual hunting plan for the suspicion of or the occurrence of an infectious animal disease subject to the obligation to control under the provisions of the Law of 11 March 2004 on the protection of animals and on the control of infectious animal diseases - this will allow for increasing the firing during the hunting year - secondly, the possibility to terminate the lease agreement for the hunting circle which, in an unjustified manner, does not implement the annual hunting plan at least 80% of the minimum number of fat game laid down in that plan to be obtained in each of the three consecutive hunting years or in the case of a negative assessment of the conduct of the hunting circle concerned. Thank you very much.

The proposed draft therefore does not properly implement the judgment of the Constitutional Tribunal of 10 July 2014, in which the Court ruled that the existing rules are non-constitutional, since the owners of the property belonging to the hunting area do not have any legal means to object to the pursuit of hunting on the land which is the subject of their ownership. The provisions of the proposed amendment are also non-constitutional, so the legal situation would not improve at all. The disclosure of the world view and religious affiliation is subject to the Personal Data Protection Act of 1997, adopted in connection with the international obligations of the Republic of Poland. The need to demonstrate a real relationship with the worldview is not needed to achieve the social objectives of the hunting economy. Point 2 of Article 2 5 states that the Minister of the Environment will, by regulation, establish a list of wild game species, taking into account inter alia. cultivating hunting traditions. This threatens the main role of hunting, i.e. the protection and management of wild game in accordance with the principles of ecology and the principles of rational farming, forestry and fishing. This provision would lower the standards for the protection of wild animals. The EU's precautionary principle is reflected in Polish law, in the Environmental Protection Act, which states that anyone undertaking activities whose negative environmental impacts are not yet fully recognised is obliged to take all possible preventive measures, based on caution. The development of this provision is Article. 47 Environmental Impact Assessment Act. A strategic environmental impact assessment shall also be required for projects other than those referred to in Article. 46. Therefore, it is necessary to adapt the Hunting Law to those provisions which the proposed proposal does not do. In the light of the project, the multi-annual breeding plans form the basis for the pursuit of the hunting economy. They consist, among others. the management and collection of wild game populations. The project allows the adoption of multi-annual hunting breeding plans without carrying out a strategic environmental impact assessment. Multiannual breeding plans are documents and meet the criteria needed to create an obligation to draw up a strategic environmental impact assessment. Multi-annual breeding plans in the light of Article. 8b as proposed in the project will include estimates of the number of animals. Unreliable inventory of these animals can lead to harmful hunting plans, which may result in excessive wildfire and the collapse of the predator population. Such cases have already been known in the near past. In particular, collective hunting, which may form part of the implementation of hunting plans, has a direct impact on the areas of protection. In particular, they lead to an apprehension of other animals in the hunting area, including protected species. Ladies and gentlemen, considering all of them. St. Barbara, when she ran away, prayed warmly for her rescue. God answered her request. Among the rocks, Barbara found an underground cave where she found refuge. ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u634	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Today's statement I would like to dedicate to the Abbey of the Benedictine Monks in the United States in the Great District. The Benedictine Abbey in Staniątki was founded in 1216 and celebrates the 800th anniversary this year. The celebration of the round anniversary lasts all year. The main celebrations took place on Sunday, 18 September. The solemn Mass of St. presided over the Prefect of the Congregation for Foreign Affairs. Institutes of Consecrated Life and Associations of Apostolic Life. Numerous representatives of the episcopate took part in the celebrations. The priest's patronage over the Jubilee was taken over by the metropolitan of Kraków cardinal. Stanisław Dziwisz, and the honorary patronage - President of the Republic of Poland Andrzej Duda. The Benedictine Sisters monastery in Staniątki is the oldest Benedictine monastery in Poland. It was funded by Clement Jaks Gryphy for the daughter of Wizenna. The monastery was equipped by the founder with extensive properties, from which to this day there was nothing left, except a large garden. The abbey flourished in the 17th century when there were about 50 sisters in the monastery. Between 1619 and 1649 the monastery was expanded, but later during the Swedish Deluge it was seriously destroyed. In 1784, at the abbey, a government school for ladies and a school for rural children was opened. The schools developed in the 19th century. After World War II, the authorities of the Polish People's Republic eliminated the monastery school run by the sisters, took the land from the monks and moved it to the monastery in Alvernia. Currently in Poland there are nine Benedictine monasteries, and the religious community in Staniątki has 18 sisters. The monastery in Staniątki is also a Marian sanctuary in which Our Lady of Pains is worshiped. In 1924 her painting was crowned with papal crowns by Cardinal Adam Stefan Sapieha of Cracow. In the monastery there are rich collections of art and artistic crafts. Among the most precious treasures of the abbey are among others. The late Gothic sculpture of Christ Frasoble, a collection of portraits of the monastery's monasteries and numerous liturgical paraments. The monastery building in Staniątki, in which the sisters live related to the rule of St. Benedicta, it's very extensive. Keeping it requires a lot of money. For this reason, the nuns, in order to maintain the abbey, lead a guest house, sell cultivated at the monastery pond of carp, distribute preparations and gingerbread, and during the celebration of All Saints - chrysanthemums. In addition, they promise a prayer memory for the dead of the family of the person who purchased flowers from the sisters. On the occasion of the 800th anniversary of the Benedictine Abbey in Staniątki, I wish all dear sisters the serenity, the joy of service and many kindness from people. May your service always be a noble testimony to modern society. The 800th anniversary celebrations will close next Sunday. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u638	A statement by the Members on the 70th anniversary of the death of Victor Zacheus Nowowiejski. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On December 6, exactly 70 years ago, one of the soldiers of the underworld of independence Victor Zacheusz Nowowiejski died. A hedgehog. To honor this hero's memory, I'm going to give him a brief overview of his life story. Victor Zacheusz Nowowiejski was born on 28 December 1915 in the village of Zembrzus in Mazury. He came from a small-scale family that cultivated patriotic traditions. He started his education at a community school in Janów, then studied at a teacher's seminary in Mława. He graduated in 1936, and soon after that he began military service at a school in Zambrów. Later he worked as a primary school teacher. The outbreak of the war in September 1939 was no surprise to him. He lived and worked in a belt bordering the Prusai, thus witnessing the preparation of the fifth column for armed activities. As a corporal, he received a mobilization card for the unit in Biała Podlaski and participated in the defensive war in 1939. After the surrender of the army and the successful escape from the POW camp, he returned to his home in the late autumn. At that time he and his colleagues began to organize secret teaching and to form structures for the armed underground. Victor Zacchaeus was a member of the Armed Fighting Union. In the spring of 1942, he managed to avoid arrest by the Gestapo. He was warned earlier and fled with false documents to the district of Nidzicki. In April 1943. Nowywiejski was sworn in as a soldier of the Home Army under the ps. Yeah, that's right. His task was to collect messages through a network of informants for the headquarters of the Olsztyn - Tuchola AK. After a few months, he was part of the guerrilla unit led by Stefan Rudziński. Victor. He became famous for many really bravado actions against Germany, which were carried out in the former Eastern Prussia. In the ranks of the Nowowiejski branch, he liquidated German police stations and also removed agents who cooperated with the occupation. In addition, he organized material aid for the families of men who were imprisoned or sent for forced labor. After the Soviets entered the area of the Przasny district, he did not decide to lay down his weapon. Together with other guerrillas, he held actions in which he defended those oppressed by the Communists. One of the loudest actions took place on the night of 2rd and 3rd June 1945 in Mława, where the guerrillas freed about 30 people, mainly former soldiers and officers of the National Army from the prison in Ubeck. For this reason, numerous manhunts were organized. His family was also persecuted. In November 1945. Nowowiejski returned to his hometown and became commander of one of the guerrilla troops of the Freedom and Independence Association. Shortly thereafter, on December 6, 1946, a group of UB officers from Przysnysz, supported by a platoon of soldiers from 3. The Ulanów Warszawski Regiment led to the death of Nowowiejski in Zembrzus, near his own home. When the bullet hit him, he tried to run away. The moment he realized that escape was not possible, he took his life with a shot to the head. Her body was taken to Przasnysz, and despite many requests it was not given to the family. To this day, the place of his burial is unknown. Hello and praise the heroes! Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2016-12-01-sejm-31-3.u639	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On 3 December, we are celebrating the International Day of Persons with Disabilities, which was established in 1992 by the UN General Assembly, which aims not only to pay particular attention to disability and related problems, but also to raise public awareness of the benefits of removing barriers and to create a common framework for people with different fitness categories. How wise and mature civil society is, it is reflected in its attitude towards those who, without the help of others, cannot cope in a world full of barriers to them, such as architectural, communication, information, difficulty in finding work and stereotypical perception of people with disabilities. Let us remember that people with disabilities, by providing the right conditions, realise their potential in the same way as people with disabilities. On International Day of Persons with Disabilities, but also on any other day of the year, let us build a society that is sensitive to the needs of these people, let us do our utmost to enable them to participate actively in economic, social and political life. From this unique place, I would like to wish all those with disabilities and their families lovingly. Above all, I wish you much health, much joy in everyday life and human kindness.
